People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I'm tired all the time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned waking up shaking and checking the door—are these nightmares or bad dreams, and do you remember what they're about?\n\nPatient: It's... they aren't really nightmares. I'm actually awake when it happens. I check the door because... well, I hear a woman’s voice coming from the corner of my room. She tells me that I ruined everything and that I should just stay inside. It's not a dream or just a thought. It sounds exactly as clear as if someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: When you hear this woman's voice telling you to stay inside, do you believe it's a real person speaking to you, or do you know it's not real but you hear it anyway?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... I know I'm the only one in the house. There shouldn't be anyone else in the room with me. But it sounds exactly like a real person is standing right there talking to me. So... I don't really know what it is, I just know I hear her.\n\nDoctor: Besides hearing this woman's voice, do you have any other beliefs or experiences that seem unusual—for example, do you feel like people are watching you, or that things happening around you have special meanings directed at you, or do you experience any other voices or sounds?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know it sounds impossible, but yes. I'm pretty sure the people downstairs, and some of my ex's friends, have been tracking when I leave the apartment. It's just... the hallway lights will blink right after I even think about going to the store. That's how I know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the hallway lights blinking and people tracking you—do you believe this is definitely happening, or do you have any doubt that it might not be real?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really have any doubt. Like I said, I know it sounds impossible to someone else, but I'm sure it's happening. The people downstairs and my ex's friends are tracking me. I mean, the lights in the hallway blink right after I even just think about going to the store. That can't just be a coincidence. It's how I know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely—like a close family member or friend—checked your apartment and the hallway and found no evidence of tracking devices or people watching you, would that change your mind about what's happening, or would you still be convinced they're tracking you?\n\nPatient: I... I mean, even if someone I trusted checked, I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds impossible, like I said, but I'm just sure it's happening. The people downstairs and my ex's friends are tracking when I leave. They wouldn't need obvious devices anyway. The hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store... that's how I know. I just know they're doing it, no matter what someone else finds.\n\nDoctor: When you first started hearing the woman's voice in your room and noticing the hallway lights blinking—did these experiences begin around the same time as your depression started, or did they come later after you were already feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: They've mostly been happening at the exact same time. For a lot of this past year, it's just been... both together. Like, I would be lying in bed, feeling completely hopeless and just unable to get up, and right while I'm feeling like that, I'm also hearing that woman telling me I ruined everything. So yeah, the low mood and the voices have just been going on together.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. That's how I know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the hallway lights blinking and people tracking you—do you believe this is definitely happening, or do you have any doubt that it might not be real?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really have any doubt. Like I said, I know it sounds impossible to someone else, but I'm sure it's happening. The people downstairs and my ex's friends are tracking me. I mean, the lights in the hallway blink right after I even just think about going to the store. That can't just be a coincidence. It's how I know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely—like a close family member or friend—checked your apartment and the hallway and found no evidence of tracking devices or people watching you, would that change your mind about what's happening, or would you still be convinced they're tracking you?\n\nPatient: I... The people downstairs and my ex's friends are tracking when I leave. They wouldn't need obvious devices anyway. The hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store... that's how I know. I just know they're doing it, no matter what someone else finds.\n\nDoctor: When you first started hearing the woman's voice in your room and noticing the hallway lights blinking—did these experiences begin around the same time as your depression started, or did they come later after you were already feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: They've mostly been happening at the exact same time. For a lot of this past year, it's just been... both together. Like, I would be lying in bed, feeling completely hopeless and just unable to get up, and right while I'm feeling like that, I'm also hearing that woman telling me I ruined everything. So yeah, the low mood and the voices have just been going on together.\n\nDoctor: Before your divorce, or even going back further in your life, have you ever had a period of several weeks or months where you felt very depressed AND also experienced unusual things like hearing voices or having strong beliefs that people were watching or tracking you?\n\nPatient: No, not before the divorce. I never had anything like this happen before that. It all really started shortly after my husband and I split up. So... I guess this has been going on for about a year and eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of days or weeks where you felt unusually energetic, confident, or 'up'—where you needed less sleep than normal but still felt rested, or where you were much more active or talkative than your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt like that. So it's been lasting for about a year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Besides losing interest in activities, have you noticed changes in your sleep patterns, appetite, or energy levels since the depression started—things like sleeping much more or less than usual, eating significantly different amounts, or feeling unusually tired or fatigued?\n\nPatient: My sleep is really bad. I just... I lie awake for hours. I keep having to check the door, and I just lie there listening to make sure nothing is coming. Then, when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up way before dawn. I wake up shaking, and I just can't get back to sleep after that. My energy is just gone because of it. I'm tired all the time.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned waking up shaking and checking the door—are these nightmares or bad dreams, and do you remember what they're about?\n\nPatient: It's... they aren't really nightmares. I'm actually awake when it happens. I check the door because... well, I hear a woman’s voice coming from the corner of my room. She tells me that I ruined everything and that I should just stay inside. It's not a dream or just a thought. It sounds exactly as clear as if someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: When you hear this woman's voice telling you to stay inside, do you believe it's a real person speaking to you, or do you know it's not real but you hear it anyway?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... I know I'm the only one in the house. There shouldn't be anyone else in the room with me. But it sounds exactly like a real person is standing right there talking to me. So... I don't really know what it is, I just know I hear her.\n\nDoctor: Besides hearing this woman's voice, do you have any other beliefs or experiences that seem unusual—for example, do you feel like people are watching you, or that things happening around you have special meanings directed at you, or do you experience any other voices or sounds?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know it sounds impossible, but yes. I'm pretty sure the people downstairs, and some of my ex's friends, have been tracking when I leave the apartment. It's just... the hallway lights will blink right after I even think about going to the store. That's how I know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the hallway lights blinking and people tracking you—do you believe this is definitely happening, or do you have any doubt that it might not be real?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really have any doubt. Like I said, I know it sounds impossible to someone else, but I'm sure it's happening. The people downstairs and my ex's friends are tracking me. I mean, the lights in the hallway blink right after I even just think about going to the store. That can't just be a coincidence. It's how I know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely—like a close family member or friend—checked your apartment and the hallway and found no evidence of tracking devices or people watching you, would that change your mind about what's happening, or would you still be convinced they're tracking you?\n\nPatient: I... I mean, even if someone I trusted checked, I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds impossible, like I said, but I'm just sure it's happening. The people downstairs and my ex's friends are tracking when I leave. They wouldn't need obvious devices anyway. The hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store... that's how I know. I just know they're doing it, no matter what someone else finds.\n\nDoctor: When you first started hearing the woman's voice in your room and noticing the hallway lights blinking—did these experiences begin around the same time as your depression started, or did they come later after you were already feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: They've mostly been happening at the exact same time. For a lot of this past year, it's just been... both together. Like, I would be lying in bed, feeling completely hopeless and just unable to get up, and right while I'm feeling like that, I'm also hearing that woman telling me I ruined everything. So yeah, the low mood and the voices have just been going on together.\n\nDoctor: Before your divorce, or even going back further in your life, have you ever had a period of several weeks or months where you felt very depressed AND also experienced unusual things like hearing voices or having strong beliefs that people were watching or tracking you?\n\nPatient: No, not before the divorce. I never had anything like this happen before that. It all really started shortly after my husband and I split up. So... I guess this has been going on for about a year and eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of days or weeks where you felt unusually energetic, confident, or 'up'—where you needed less sleep than normal but still felt rested, or where you were much more active or talkative than your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt like that. Honestly, it's usually the exact opposite. I... I never really have times where I feel super energetic or confident. And I definitely need my sleep, even if I'm just lying there feeling awful. So no, I haven't ever had a period where I felt really 'up' or talked a lot more than usual.\n\nDoctor: When you first started hearing the woman's voice and noticing the hallway lights blinking, had you already been feeling depressed and hopeless for a while before that started, or did the voice and the lights begin before you felt the deep depression set in?\n\nPatient: Actually... there was a stretch before the worst of the depression really set in. I was still managing to cook sometimes, and I would still answer my friends when they checked on me. But I was already hearing the voice then. And I had already been believing that the hallway lights were signals about me for a few weeks before the hopeless feeling got really bad. So those things definitely started before I completely crashed.\n\nDoctor: From the time you first heard the woman's voice until now—about a year and four months—has the voice been there constantly, or have there been periods of weeks or months where the voice went away completely before coming back?\n\nPatient: Well, the voices and the beliefs about the lights... they haven't ever gone away. They started a few months after the divorce, and they've been there the whole time since. So, they've been going on for about a year and eight months straight. There hasn't been any break from them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
